. This term requires special consideration, as discussed in Remark
4.3
: in some occasions, it is estimated exactly as in Lemma 5.2 of
[
6
]
, i.e., for
n\leq K

\epsilon^{-1}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda)=I^{n}_{+}}\Big]\lesssim{}_{n}^{\epsilon}(\lambda),

(4.18)

while in other cases a different treatment is necessary. For this reason, we keep the formulation in its most general form.
The integral inequality (
4.10
) can be estimated as

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x},t)|\lesssim\sum_{\underline{y}}\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}(\underline{x}\overset{t-t^{*}}{\rightarrow}\underline{y})|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{y},t^{*})|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\sum_{q=0}^{K}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{z}^{\prime}\subset I_{+},\\
|\underline{z}^{\prime}|=q\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset\{1,\dots,n\},\\
2\leq p\leq\min\{n,K\}\end{subarray}}{{}_{n}^{\epsilon}(\lambda)}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[|v_{n-p+q}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(J)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{z}^{\prime}\subset I_{+},\\
q\geq 1\end{subarray}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j}(\lambda)\in I_{+}}|{v_{n-1+q}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\Big]}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda)=I^{n}_{+}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\bigg(\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[\epsilon^{-2}\left\{|(A^{1}v^{\epsilon}_{n})(\underline{x}(\lambda),t-\lambda)|+|(A^{2}v^{\epsilon}_{n})(\underline{x}(\lambda),t-\lambda)|\right\}\Big].

(4.19)

Here
A^{1}
and
A^{2}
are defined in (
4.5
),
p=|J|
and
q=|\underline{z}^{\prime}|
. It is worth noting that a similar line of analysis is employed in the proof of the space-time
v
-estimate in Theorem
2.24
, presented in Section
5
, where additionally the initial condition must be carefully treated.

Remark 4.4

(
A few comments on (
4.0.3
)).

We discuss a few important points regarding (
4.0.3
):

•

The indicators
\mathbf{1}_{\emptyset\neq\underline{x}^{(J)}(\lambda)\subset I_{\pm}^{c}}
appearing in (
4.0.3
) do not appear anymore in the second line of (
4.0.3
) because they are simply bounded by 1.

•

We have distinguished the scenarios that one particle in
\underline{x}
reaches the boundary, dies and a new particle is born from all the other scenarios in
B_{+}
. The former scenarios have been retained in their original form as defined in (
4.6
) while the later ones have been estimated as in (
4.0.3
).

•

This decomposition is not essential for proving Theorem
2.19
.
As shown in Section
4.1
, the last three terms in (
4.0.3
)
are estimated using Liggett’s inequality together with Gaussian kernel bounds
(see Lemma
C.7
and the estimates in Appendix
B
).
Specifically, these terms yield an estimate of the form
{}_{p}^{\epsilon}(\lambda)
for
p\geq 1
.

Remark 4.5
.

For
p\geq 2
, the factors
{}_{p}^{\epsilon}(\lambda)
in (
4.0.3
) are not integrable over the time interval
[0,t]
, i.e., when
t^{*}=0
. In general, throughout this work, whenever the time exponent in the denominator is equal or exceeds 1– e.g.
\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{k}+1}
or
\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}(t-\lambda)]^{k}+1}
with
k\geq 1
– we apply the same technique used in the proof of Lemma
C.7
to reduce the exponent to a value strictly less than 1 and make these factors integrable over the time interval
[0,t]
. We will refer to the next estimation at several points later in the paper:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{k}+1}\leq\epsilon+\int_{\epsilon^{2}}^{t}d\lambda\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1+\zeta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon+\epsilon^{1+2\zeta}t^{-\zeta}+\epsilon^{1+2\zeta}\epsilon^{-2\zeta}

(4.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c(\epsilon+\epsilon^{1+2\zeta}t^{-\zeta})\leq c\epsilon.

4.0.4
Estimation of the right-hand side of (
4.10
): two particles meet and only one dies.

We now turn our attention to the scenario that two particles meet and only one dies. This is translated in the operator
A^{1}v_{n}^{\epsilon}
, whose definition is given in (
4.5
).
Note that
A^{1}
depends on the discrete gradient of a
v
-function with one particle less, i.e.,

v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i)},t)-v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j)},t),

where
\underline{x}^{(i)}
and
\underline{x}^{(j)}
are the configuration obtained by erasing from
\underline{x}
the
i
-th and the
j
-th particles, respectively. Recalling the formula (
4.9
) for the difference of the
v
-functions, and by enlarging the space so that we can take the same expectation for the two
v
-functions, we write

\begin{split}v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i)},t)-v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j)},t)&={\bf E}_{\epsilon}\left[v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i)}(t-t^{*}),t^{*})-v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j)}(t-t^{*}),t^{*})\right]\\
&+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}\,d\lambda\,{\bf E}_{\epsilon}\left[(C_{\epsilon}v_{n-1}^{\epsilon})(\underline{x}^{(i)}(\lambda),t-\lambda)-(C_{\epsilon}v_{n-1}^{\epsilon})(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda),t-\lambda)\right].\end{split}

(4.21)

By introducing a proper stopping time
\tau_{i,j}
given in Definition
C.5
(further details can be found in Appendix
C
and Section 5 in
[
6
]
), and noting that the expectations above are acting on antisymmetric functions, from Lemma 4.3 of
[
6
]
, we can get the bound: for
n\geq 2

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt|v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i)},t)-v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j)},t)|\lesssim\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}\left(\tau_{i,j}>\frac{t-t^{*}}{2}\right)\sup_{\underline{x}\in\Lambda_{N}}|v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x},t^{*})|{\bf 1}_{\{t^{*}>0\}}

(4.22)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 3}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}\}}\epsilon^{-2}\left\{|(Av^{\epsilon}_{n-1})(\underline{x}^{(i)}(\lambda),t-\lambda)|+|(Av^{\epsilon}_{n-1})(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda),t-\lambda)|\right\}\Big]

\displaystyle+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\sum_{\underline{z}^{\prime}\subset I_{\pm}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset\{1,\dots,n-1\},\\
1\leq p\leq\min\{n-1,K\}\end{subarray}}\big(1-\mathbf{1}_{q=0,p=1}\big){{}_{p}^{\epsilon}(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\emptyset\neq\underline{x}^{(J)}(\lambda)\subset I_{+}^{c}}|v_{n-p+q}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(J)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\Big]

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 3}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{\underline{y}=\underline{x}^{(i)},\underline{x}^{(j)}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{y}}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}\}}{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda)=I^{n-1}_{+}}\Big]

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\beta^{\epsilon}_{n-1}(\underline{x}^{(i)},\underline{x}^{(j)},t;t^{*})+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 3}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{\underline{y}=\underline{x}^{(i)},\underline{x}^{(j)}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{y}}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}\}}{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda)=I^{n-1}_{+}}\Big],

where
\beta^{\epsilon}_{n-1}(\underline{x}^{(i)},\underline{x}^{(j)},t;t^{*})
is defined by the first four lines. Due to the indicator present in the second term of the right-hand side of (
4.22
), when
\underline{x}=(x_{1},x_{2})
, the following inequality holds

|v_{1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(1)},t)-v_{1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(2)},t)|\lesssim\beta^{\epsilon}_{1}(\underline{x}^{(1)},\underline{x}^{(2)},t;t^{*}).

(4.23)

Now, we use (
4.22
) and we are able to bound the expectation of
A^{1}
appearing in (
4.0.3
), namely
\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[|(A^{1}v^{\epsilon}_{n})(\underline{x}(\lambda),t-\lambda)|\right]
, by:

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\big[{}_{0}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)\beta^{\epsilon}_{n-1}(\underline{x}^{(i)},\underline{x}^{(j)},t-\lambda;t^{*})|\big]+\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[{}_{1}^{\epsilon,n-1}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)\Big],

(4.24)

where for fixed
t\in[0,T]
,
n\geq 2
,
i,j\subset\{1,\dots,n\}
and
0\leq\lambda<t
we used the notation

\displaystyle{}_{0}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\{|x_{i}(\lambda)-x_{j}(\lambda)|=1\}}\epsilon^{-2}\left|\rho_{\epsilon}(t-\lambda,x_{i}(\lambda))-\rho_{\epsilon}(t-\lambda,x_{j}(\lambda))\right|

(4.25)

and

\displaystyle{}_{1}^{\epsilon,n}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda):=\epsilon^{-1}{}_{0}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\sum_{\underline{y}=\underline{x}^{(i)}(\lambda),\,\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)}{\bf E}_{\epsilon,\underline{y}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\tau}{2}\}}{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\tau)=I_{+}^{n}}\right].

(4.26)

Moreover, in the following we will also introduce

\displaystyle{}_{2}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda):={}_{0}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)\left|\rho_{\epsilon}(t-\lambda,x_{i}(\lambda))-\rho_{\epsilon}(t-\lambda,x_{j}(\lambda))\right|.

(4.27)

4.0.5
Wrapping up.

We finish this subsection by introducing all key quantities, characterizing different scenarios in (
4.0.3
)–(
4.22
) which will be estimated in Section
4.0.6
.

The integral inequality (
4.0.3
) along with (
4.24
) and the notations introduced in (
4.25
)-(
4.27
), takes the form:

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x},t)|\lesssim\sum_{\underline{y}}\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}(\underline{x}\overset{t-t^{*}}{\rightarrow}\underline{y})|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{y},t^{*})|

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\sum_{\emptyset\neq\underline{z}^{\prime}\subset I_{\pm}}\;\;\;\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset\{1,\dots,n\},\\
2\leq p\leq\min\{n,K\}\end{subarray}}\,{}^{\epsilon}_{p}(\lambda)\,\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[v_{n-p+q}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(J)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+{\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j}(\lambda)\in I_{+}}|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j)}\cup z^{\prime},t-\lambda)|\right]}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{z}^{\prime}\subset I_{+}\\
q\geq 2\end{subarray}}{{}_{1}^{\epsilon}}(\lambda)\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[|v_{n-1+q}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathbf{1}_{2\leq n\leq K}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda)=I^{n}_{+}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[{}_{0}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)\beta_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i)}(\lambda),\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda),t-\lambda,{t^{*}})\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 3}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[{}_{1}^{\epsilon,n-1}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[{}_{2}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)|v^{\epsilon}_{n-2}(\underline{x}^{(i,j)}(\lambda),t-\lambda)|\Big].

(4.28)

The terms in second-fifth line correspond to all terms included in
B_{+}
grouped as

(a)

Second line: scenarios with more than two particles reaching the boundary and one or more new particles are born

(b)

Third line: scenarios with exactly one particle reaches the boundary and only one new particle is born (thus overall the number of particles in the system remains the same)

(c)

Fourth line: scenarios where exactly one particle reaches the boundary and at least two new particles are born

(d)

Fifth line: all particles reach the last
n
sites in the boundary, no newborns.

Definition 4.6
.

Fix
t\in[0,T]
. Let
n\geq 2
,
i,j\subset\{1,\dots,n\}
and
0<\lambda<t
. We define

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\widetilde{\gamma}_{0}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}\}}{}_{0}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)

(4.29)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}_{2}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}\}}{}_{2}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)

(4.30)

and for
1\leq p\leq\min\{n,K\}

\widetilde{\gamma}_{p}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda)=\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}\}}{}_{p}^{\epsilon}(\lambda).

(4.31)

The integral inequality (
4.22
) for
n
particles (instead of
n-1
) takes the form: for
\underline{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n+1})

\displaystyle|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i)},t;t^{*})-v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j)},t,t^{*})|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i)},\underline{x}^{(j)},t;t^{*})

(4.32)

\displaystyle+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{\underline{y}=\underline{x}^{(i)},\underline{x}^{(j)}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{y}}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}\}}{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda)=I^{n}_{+}}\Big],

where the integral inequality for
\beta_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i)},\underline{x}^{(j)},t;t^{*})
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\beta_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i)},\underline{x}^{(j)},t;t^{*})\lesssim\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}\left(\tau_{i,j}>\frac{t-t^{*}}{2}\right)\sup_{\underline{x}\in\Lambda^{n,\neq}_{N}}|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x},t^{*})|{\bf 1}_{\{t^{*}>0\}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\neq i}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[\widetilde{\gamma}_{0}^{\epsilon}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime},\lambda,t-\lambda)\left|v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i,i^{\prime})}(\lambda),t-\lambda)-v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i,j^{\prime})}(\lambda),t-\lambda)\right|\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\neq j}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[\widetilde{\gamma}_{0}^{\epsilon}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime},\lambda,t-\lambda)\left|v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j,i^{\prime})}(\lambda),t-\lambda)-v_{n-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j,j^{\prime})}(\lambda),t-\lambda)\right|\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\neq i}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[\widetilde{\gamma}_{2}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime},\lambda,t-\lambda)|v_{n-2}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i,i^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(\lambda),t-\lambda)|\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\neq i}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[\widetilde{\gamma}_{2}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime},\lambda,t-\lambda)|v_{n-2}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j,i^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(\lambda),t-\lambda)|\Big]

\displaystyle+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}\!\!\!\!\!d\lambda\!\!\!\!\!\;\sum_{\underline{z}^{\prime}\subset I_{\pm}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset\{1,\dots,n\},\\
1\leq p\leq\min\{n,K\}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\big(1-\mathbf{1}_{q=0,p=1}\big)\widetilde{\gamma}_{p}^{\epsilon}(\lambda)\,\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{\underline{x}^{(J)}(\lambda)\neq I^{n}_{+}}|v_{n}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(J)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\Big].

(4.33)

4.0.6
Useful estimates

In this section, we estimate the expectation of
{}^{\epsilon}_{0}
,
{}_{1}^{\epsilon,n}
and
{}_{2}^{\epsilon}
which play a key role in deriving the integral inequalities for the space
v
-function.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
i,j\subset\{1,\dots,n\}
,
\zeta>0
,
\epsilon<1
and
\underline{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
. Under
Assumption 1
given in (
2.21
), for any
0\leq\lambda\leq t
, the following estimates hold:

\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}}[{}^{{\epsilon}}_{0}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)]\lesssim{\phi_{0}(\lambda)}:=\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/2+\zeta}},

(4.34)

\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}}[{}_{2}^{{\epsilon}}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)]\lesssim\,{\phi^{\epsilon}_{2}(\lambda):=}\epsilon\left(\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{3/4}}\right).

(4.35)

Moreover, for
n\geq 3
,

\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}}[{}^{{\epsilon},n-1}_{1}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)]\lesssim{\phi^{\epsilon,n-1}_{1}(\lambda)}:=\epsilon\phi_{0}(\lambda)\mathbf{1}_{n=3}+\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]+1}\epsilon^{-4\zeta}\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 4},

(4.36)

where
{}^{\epsilon}_{0}
,
{}_{1}^{\epsilon,n-1}
and
{}^{\epsilon}_{2}
are defined in (
4.25
)–(
4.27
).

Proof.

First, we estimate (
4.34
). By Lemma
2.12
and Liggett’s inequality (
C.7
), we bound
\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}[{}_{0}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)]
from above by a constant times:

\displaystyle{\bf E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{|x_{i}(\lambda)-x_{j}(\lambda)|=1\}}\left(\epsilon+\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{z\in I_{\pm}}G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau}(x_{i}(\lambda),z)\right)\right]

\displaystyle{\lesssim\sum_{\pi\in S_{n}}\sum_{w}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(x_{\pi(i)},w)P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(x_{\pi(j)},w+1)\left(\epsilon+\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{z\in I_{\pm}}G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau}(w,z)\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{\pi\in S_{n}}\sum_{w}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(x_{\pi(i)},w)\left(\epsilon+\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{z\in I_{\pm}}G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau}(w,z)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\epsilon+\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{\pi\in S_{n}}\sum_{z\in I_{\pm}}G_{\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda+\tau)}(x_{\pi(i)},z)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\epsilon+\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda+\tau)]^{1/2}+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\epsilon+\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2+\zeta}}\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2-\zeta}},

where
S_{n}
is the set of all permutations of
\{1,\dots,n\}
. To obtain
G_{\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda+\tau)}(x_{\pi(i)},z)
, we use (
B.5
), i.e.,

\sum_{w}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(x_{\pi(i)},w)G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau}(w,z)\lesssim\sum_{w}G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau}(x_{\pi(i)},w)G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau}(w,z)=G_{\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda+\tau)}(x_{\pi(i)},z),

followed by Kolmogorov’s identity. Furthermore, in the last inequality, we use that
\lambda+\tau>\max\{\tau,\lambda\}
which implies that

\frac{1}{(\lambda+\tau)^{1/2}}=\frac{1}{(\lambda+\tau)^{1/2-\zeta}}\frac{1}{(\lambda+\tau)^{\zeta}}<\frac{1}{\tau^{1/2-\zeta}}\frac{1}{\lambda^{\zeta}}.

Then the estimation follows directly.

Now we proceed estimating (
4.35
). We can bound
\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}[{}^{\epsilon}_{2}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)]
by a constant times

\displaystyle{\bf E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{|x_{i}(\lambda)-x_{j}(\lambda)|=1\}}\left(\epsilon+\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{z\in I_{\pm}}G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau}(x_{i}(\lambda),z)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim{\bf E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{|x_{i}(\lambda)-x_{j}(\lambda)|=1\}}\left(\epsilon^{2}+\left(\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{z\in I_{\pm}}G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau}(x_{i}(\lambda),z)\right)^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\pi\in S_{n}}\sum_{w}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(x_{\pi(i)},w)P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(x_{\pi(j)},{w+1})\bigg(\epsilon^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau_{1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{t-\lambda}d\tau_{2}\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{1}]^{1/2}+1}\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{2}]^{1/2}+1}{\sum_{z_{1}\in I_{\pm}}G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{1}}(w,z_{1})\sum_{z_{2}\in I_{\pm}}G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{2}}(w,z_{2})\bigg)}.

We note now that the contribution of the transition probabilities can be combined with the heat kernels in the following fashion:

\displaystyle\sum_{w}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(x_{\pi(i)},w)P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(x_{\pi(j)},{w+1})\sum_{z_{1}\in I_{\pm}}G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{1}}(w,z_{1})\sum_{z_{2}\in I_{\pm}}G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{2}}(w,z_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{z_{1}\in I_{\pm}}\sum_{z_{2}\in I_{\pm}}\underbrace{\sum_{w}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(x_{\pi(i)},w)G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{1}}(w,z_{1})}_{\lesssim G_{\epsilon^{-2}(\tau_{1}+\lambda)}({x_{\pi(i)}},z_{1})}\underbrace{P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(x_{\pi(j)},{w+1})}_{\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}}\underbrace{G_{\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{2}}(w,z_{2})}_{\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{2}]^{1/2}+1}},

from where it follows that the last display is bounded from above by a constant times

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}}\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau_{1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{t-\lambda}d\tau_{2}\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{1}]^{1/2}+1}\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{2}]+1}{\sum_{\pi\in S_{n}}}\sum_{z_{1}\in I_{\pm}}G_{\epsilon^{-2}(\tau_{1}+\lambda)}({x_{\pi(i)}},z_{1}).

We now estimate the rightmost term in the last line of the last display: we first bound the heat kernel as
G_{\epsilon^{-2}(\tau_{1}+\lambda)}(x_{\pi(i)},z)\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda+\tau_{1})]^{1/2}}
, then use that

\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda+\tau_{1})]^{1/2}}\leq\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/4}}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{1}]^{1/4}},

because
\lambda+\tau_{1}>\tau_{1}
,
\lambda+\tau_{1}>\lambda
and
\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}
, and

\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{2}]}=\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{2}]^{1-\zeta}}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{2}]^{\zeta}}\leq\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{2}]^{1-\zeta}}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{1}]^{\zeta}}.

Note that the exponents of
(\lambda+\tau_{1})^{-1/2}
and
\tau_{2}^{-1/2}
can be distributed in different ways to ensure that the integrand in the double integral remains integrable. The choices presented above constitute one such example that satisfies this condition. Overall, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}}\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau_{1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{t-\lambda}d\tau_{2}\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{1}]^{1/2}+1}\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau_{2}]+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda+\tau_{1})]^{1/2}}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{\lambda^{3/4}}.

We now proceed with estimating (
4.36
).
First, it is straightforward to check that when
n=3
the integral in time appearing in the expectation of
{}_{1}^{n-1}
takes the form

\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\,{\bf E}_{\epsilon,y}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\tau}{2}}{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\tau)=(N-1,N)}\right]\lesssim\epsilon^{2}

because we first condition on
\mathcal{F}(\frac{\tau}{2})
, being the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the active/passive marks in the interval
[0,\frac{\tau}{2})
, then estimate the expectation
{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\tau)=(N-1,N)}
and finally estimate
\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\tau}{2}}
using (
4.20
); see a similar calculation in the proof of Lemma
4.8
.
To be more precise, we start with the internal integral in (
4.26
) when
n=3
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\,{\bf E}_{\epsilon,y}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\tau}{2}}{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\tau)=(N-1,N)}\right]=\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}{\bf E}_{\epsilon,y}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\tau}{2}}{\bf E}_{\epsilon}\left[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\tau)=(N-1,N)}|\mathcal{F}\left(\tfrac{\tau}{2}\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\lesssim\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\,{\bf E}_{\epsilon,y}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\tau}{2}}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\tau/2)]+1}\right]\lesssim\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\,\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\tau/2)]^{3/2}+1}\lesssim\epsilon^{2},

where the last line follows from Remark
4.5
. Now, the remaining follows from the estimates of
{}_{0}^{\epsilon}
. Putting everything together we get that for
n=3

\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}[{}_{1}^{\epsilon,n-1}(i,j,\lambda)]\overset{n=3}{=}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}[{}_{1}^{2,\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda)]\lesssim\epsilon\phi_{0}(\lambda).

For
n\geq 4
and
i,j\subset\{1,\dots,n\}
,
we need to control

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}\,d\tau\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[\epsilon^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\{|x_{i}(\lambda)-x_{j}(\lambda)|=1\}}\epsilon^{-2}\left|\rho_{\epsilon}(t-\lambda,x_{i}(\lambda))-\rho_{\epsilon}(t-\lambda,x_{j}(\lambda))\right|

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\sum_{\underline{y}=\underline{x}^{(i)}(\lambda),\,\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)}{\bf E}_{\epsilon,\underline{y}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\tau}{2}\}}{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\tau)=I_{+}^{3}}\right]\Big].

From Corollary
D.2
we can bound the previous display by a constant times

\displaystyle\epsilon^{-2-2\zeta}\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}\,d\tau\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{|x_{i}(\lambda)-x_{j}(\lambda)|=1\}}\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}d\tau\sum_{\underline{y}=\underline{x}^{(i)}(\lambda),\,\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)}{\bf E}_{\epsilon,\underline{y}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\tau}{2}\}}{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\tau)=I_{+}^{3}}\right]\right]

and now we control the time integral as follows:

\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}\,d\tau\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{|x_{i}(\lambda)-x_{j}(\lambda)|=1\}}{\bf E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(i)}(\lambda)}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\tau}{2}}{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\tau)=\{N-(n-2),\dots,N\}}\right]\right]}

\displaystyle{\lesssim\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}\,d\tau\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{|x_{i}(\lambda)-x_{j}(\lambda)|=1\}}\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(i)}(\lambda)\overset{\tau}{\longrightarrow}(N-(n-2),\dots,N)\right]}

\displaystyle={\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}\,d\tau\sum_{\underline{w}}\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}(\underline{x}\overset{\lambda}{\rightarrow}\underline{w})\mathbf{1}_{\{|w_{i}-w_{j}|=1\}}\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}(\underline{w}^{(i)}\overset{\tau}{\longrightarrow}(N-(n-2),\dots,N)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}\,d\tau\sum_{w_{1},\dots,w_{n}}\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}(\underline{x}\overset{\lambda}{\rightarrow}w_{1},\dots,w_{j},w_{j}\pm 1,\dots,w_{n})\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}(\underline{w}^{(i)}\overset{\tau}{\longrightarrow}N-(n-2),\dots,N)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}\,d\tau\sum_{\pi\in S_{n}}\prod_{k\neq i,j}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda+\tau}(x_{\pi(k)},N-(n-k))

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\sum_{w_{j}}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(x_{\pi(j)},w_{j})P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(w_{j},N-(n-j))P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(w_{j}\pm 1,N-(n-i))

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}}\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}\,d\tau\sum_{\pi\in S_{n}}\prod_{k\neq i}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda+\tau}(x_{\pi(k)},N-(n-k))\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}\,d\tau\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\tau+\lambda)]^{(n-2)/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]+1}\int_{0}^{t-\lambda}\,d\tau\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\tau+\lambda)]^{(n-3)/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]+1}\epsilon^{2-2\zeta}(t-\lambda)^{\zeta}.

In the above estimation, we bound
\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\tau}{2}}
by 1, we obtain the fourth line of the right-hand side by Liggett’s inequality given in (
C.6
), the fifth line by Kolmogorov’s identity and the sixth-seventh line by also using that
\lambda+\tau>\max\{\tau,\lambda\}
. Thus,

\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\tau+\lambda)]^{(n-2)/2}+1}\leq\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda)]^{1/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{(n-3)/2}+1}\leq\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda)]^{1/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2-\zeta}}.

For the difference of
\rho_{\epsilon}
’s in
\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}[{}_{1}^{\epsilon,n-1}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)]
,
n\geq 4
, we apply Corollary
D.2
.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
i,j\subset\{1,\dots,n\}
,
\zeta>0
and
\epsilon<1
. Under
Assumption 1
given in (
2.21
), we have:

\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}[\widetilde{\gamma}_{0}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)]\lesssim\frac{\epsilon^{1-3\zeta}}{\lambda^{1-\zeta}}

(4.37)

and

\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}[\widetilde{\gamma}^{\epsilon}_{2}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)]\lesssim\frac{\epsilon^{3/2-2\zeta}}{\lambda^{1-\zeta}}.

(4.38)

For any
J\subset\{1,\dots,n\}
and
1\leq p\leq n
,

\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}[\widetilde{\gamma}^{\epsilon}_{p}(\lambda)]\lesssim{}_{p+1}^{\epsilon}(\lambda).

(4.39)

Proof.

The proof follows the same steps as the proof of Lemma
4.7
. More precisely, for
m\in\{0,2\}
, let us denote
\tilde{\gamma}^{\epsilon}_{m}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)=\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}\}}{}_{m}^{\epsilon}(i,j,\lambda,t-\lambda)
and

\tilde{\gamma}^{\epsilon}_{p}(i,j,\lambda)=\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}\}}{}_{p}^{\epsilon}(\lambda)
.
Then the estimate goes as follows: let
\mathcal{F}(\frac{\lambda}{2})
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the active/passive marks in the interval
[0,\frac{\lambda}{2})
. Then for
m\in\{0,2\}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[\widetilde{\gamma}_{m}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}(\lambda))\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}\}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[\gamma(\underline{x}(\lambda))|\mathcal{F}\left(\tfrac{\lambda}{2}\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(\lambda)\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}}\right]\lesssim\phi_{m}(\lambda)\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon^{-2}\frac{\lambda}{2}}+1},

where we use (
C.2
). Finally, we adjust the power of
\lambda
such that the time factors are integrable, e.g., for
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}
, we work as follows:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/2+\zeta}}\right)\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\frac{\lambda}{2}]^{1/2}+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]+1}+\frac{\epsilon^{-1-\zeta}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1+\zeta}+1}\lesssim\epsilon^{1-3\zeta}\frac{1}{\lambda^{1-\zeta}}.

The same estimate holds for
\tilde{\gamma}^{\epsilon}_{p}(i,j,\lambda)
.
∎

4.0.7
Integral inequalities for space
v
-functions

Equations (
4.0.3
) and (
4.22
) become more tractable when taking the supremum over all configurations with the same number of particles. Therefore, for every
0\leq t\leq T
, we define

a_{\epsilon}(n,t):=\sup_{\underline{x}\in\Lambda_{N}^{n,\neq}}|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x},t)|\quad\textrm{with}\quad a_{\epsilon}(0,t)=1

(4.40)

and for
\underline{e}_{1}
being the unit vector in the positive 1st-direction, we define

d_{\epsilon}(n,t):=\sup_{\underline{x}\in\Lambda_{N}^{n,\neq}}|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x},t)-v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}+\underline{e}_{1},t)|\;\textrm{and\;}d_{\epsilon}(0,t)=0.

(4.41)

Similarly, we define

\beta_{\epsilon}(n,t,{t^{*}}):=\sup_{\underline{x}\in\Lambda_{N}^{n,\neq}}\beta^{\epsilon}_{n}(\underline{x},\underline{x}+\underline{e}_{1},t,{t^{*}}).

(4.42)

Remark 4.9
.

We remark that in the definition of
d
given above, it is irrelevant whether the increment is taken in the first coordinate or not: since we start from the set representation, e.g.
X=\{x,y,z\}
and
\tilde{X}=\{x,y,z+1\}
, we may always choose an ordering such as
\underline{x}=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})=(z,x,y)
so that
\underline{x}+\underline{e}_{1}=(x_{1}+1,x_{2},x_{3})=(z+1,x,y)
.
Of course, one could equally consider
\underline{x}+\underline{e}_{2}
or
\underline{x}+\underline{e}_{3}
, but the argument works in exactly the same way for any coordinate.

The first main result of this subsection is the next lemma.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
K\geq 2
be a fixed integer number,
0\leq t^{*}<t\leq T
and
\zeta>0
. Under Assumption 1 given in (
2.21
) the following integral inequality for
a_{\epsilon}(n,t)
holds: for
n\geq 1

\displaystyle a_{\epsilon}(n,t)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle a_{\epsilon}(n,t^{*})

(4.43)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1-\zeta}}\sum_{q=1}^{K}\;\;\;\sum_{p=2}^{\min\{n,K\}}\,a_{\epsilon}(n-p+q,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1-\zeta}}\sum_{p=2}^{\min\{n,K\}}\mathbf{1}_{p\neq n}\,a_{\epsilon}(n-p,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}a_{\epsilon}(n,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{q=2}^{K}a_{\epsilon}(n-1+q,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n=2}\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}(t-t^{*})^{\zeta}+\mathbf{1}_{3\leq n\leq K}\epsilon

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n=3}\epsilon\left((t-t^{*})^{1/2}+(t-t^{*})^{1/2+\zeta}\right)+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 4}\epsilon^{2-4\zeta}(t-t^{*})^{\zeta}

\displaystyle+{\bf 1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\phi_{0}(\lambda)\beta_{\epsilon}(n-1,t-\lambda,{t^{*}})

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\,\phi_{2}^{\epsilon}(\lambda)a_{\epsilon}(n-2,t-\lambda)\,.

Proof.

The derivation of (
4.43
) is mainly based on Lemma
4.7
. We first bound each
v_{n}^{\epsilon}
appearing on the right-hand side of (
4.0.5
) with its supremum with respect to all configurations with the same number of particles. We bound all
{}_{p}(\lambda)
with
2\leq p\leq\min\{n,K\}
in (
4.0.5
) by
\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1-\zeta}}
using Remark
4.5
and estimate
\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda)=I^{n}_{+}}\right]
as
\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{n/2}+1}\leq\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1-\zeta}}
,
n\leq K
. Note that the third term in the right-hand side of (
4.43
) includes the indicator function of
p\neq n
whereas the case
p=n
appears separately in the sixth term.
∎

Note that Lemma
4.10
is used in the proof of Theorem
2.19
; see Section
4.1
. However, this lemma is not enough for our purposes, hence we derive another integral inequality, which is stated in the next lemma. The idea behind this lemma is as follows: the right-hand side of (
4.0.5
) contains terms that cannot be further improved using the configuration dependence of
v_{j}^{\epsilon}
. Consequently,
v_{j}^{\epsilon}
is bounded by the supremum over all configurations with the same number of particles. In contrast, for the remaining terms, the configuration dependence plays a role and leads to sharper estimates; hence, these terms are not bounded by the supremum over all configurations with the same number of particles.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
K\geq 2
be a fixed integer number,
0\leq t^{*}<t\leq T
and
\zeta>0
. Under
Assumption 1
given in (
2.21
), the following integral inequality for
v^{\epsilon}_{n}(\underline{x},t)
holds: for
n\geq 1
and any labeled configuration
\underline{x}\in\Lambda_{N}^{\neq,n}

\displaystyle|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x},t)|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle a_{\epsilon}(n,t^{*})

(4.44)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1-\zeta}}\sum_{q=1}^{K}\;\;\;\sum_{p=2}^{\min\{n,K\}}\,a_{\epsilon}(n-p+q,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1-\zeta}}\sum_{p=2}^{\min\{n,K\}}\mathbf{1}_{p\neq n}\,a_{\epsilon}(n-p,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j}(\lambda)\in I_{+}}|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j)}{(\lambda)}\cup z^{\prime},t-\lambda)|\Big]\right]

\displaystyle+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{z}^{\prime}\subset I_{+},\\
q\geq 2\end{subarray}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[|v_{n-1+q}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\Big]\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{2\leq n\leq K}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda)=I^{n}_{+}}\right]

\displaystyle+\epsilon\left((t-t^{*})^{1/2}+(t-t^{*})^{1/2+\zeta}\right)\mathbf{1}_{n=3}+(t-t^{*})^{\zeta}\epsilon^{2-4\zeta}\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 4}

\displaystyle+{\bf 1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda{\phi_{0}(\lambda)}\beta_{\epsilon}(n-1,t-\lambda,{t^{*}})

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\,{\phi_{2}^{\epsilon}(\lambda)}a_{\epsilon}(n-2,t-\lambda)\,.

The proof is omitted being similar to the proof of Lemma
4.10
.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
K\geq 2
be a fixed integer,
0\leq t^{*}<t\leq T
and
\zeta>0
.
For
n\geq 1
, it holds:

\displaystyle d_{\epsilon}(n,t)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{2\leq n\leq K}\epsilon+\beta_{\epsilon}(n,t,{t^{*}}),

(4.45)

where

\displaystyle\beta_{\epsilon}(n,t,t^{*})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon(t-t^{*})^{-1/2}a_{\epsilon}(n,t^{*})

(4.46)

\displaystyle+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1-\zeta}}\sum_{q=1}^{K}\;\;\;\sum_{p=2}^{\min\{n,K\}}\,a_{\epsilon}(n-p+q,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1-\zeta}}\sum_{p=2}^{\min\{n,K\}}\mathbf{1}_{p\neq n}\,a_{\epsilon}(n-p,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1-\zeta}}a_{\epsilon}(n,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1-\zeta}}\sum_{q=2}^{K}a_{\epsilon}(n-1+q,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\frac{\epsilon^{3/2-2\zeta}}{\lambda^{1-\zeta}}a_{\epsilon}(n-2,t-\lambda)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 2}\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\frac{\epsilon^{1-3\zeta}}{\lambda^{1-\zeta}}d_{\epsilon}(n-1,t-\lambda).

Proof.

The integral inequality (
4.45
)–(
4.46
) is obtained by (
4.22
) and (
4.0.5
). The derivation is analogous to that of Lemma
4.11
, except that the
\gamma
-factors are replaced by modified factors
\widetilde{\gamma}
defined in (
4.29
)–(
4.31
). This is necessary since (
4.22
) includes the additional indicator
\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{i,j}>\frac{\lambda}{2}\}}
which can be estimated using Theorem
C.6
– a result originally proved in
[
6
]
. The estimates of the new factors
\widetilde{\gamma}
are given in Lemma
4.8
and are bounded by applying the same arguments used to control the expectations in Lemma
4.7
along with Remark
4.5
.
∎

4.1
Proof of Theorem
2.19

The core idea of the proof was originally developed in
[
3
]
. It boils down to showing that

\displaystyle a^{\star}_{\epsilon}(m,t)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\Gamma}_{a}^{\epsilon}(m)\sup_{0\leq\sigma\leq t}a_{\epsilon}(m,\sigma)

(4.47)

for
t=T
is bounded. Above

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{m=1,2}\epsilon^{-1+2\zeta}+\mathbf{1}_{3\leq m\leq K}\epsilon^{-1}+\mathbf{1}_{m\geq K+1}\epsilon^{-1-\zeta}.

(4.48)

To do so here, we divide the time interval
[0,T]
into
l\in\mdmathbb{Z}
, to be chosen later, equal sub-intervals
[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
with
t_{0}=0
and
t_{l+1}=T
. The length of each interval is given by
|t_{k}-t_{k-1}|=\Delta
and
\frac{T}{\Delta}=l
. For
m\geq 1
and
k=1,\dots,l+1
, we denote

\displaystyle d^{\star}_{\epsilon}(m,k)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\Gamma}_{d}^{\epsilon}(m)\sup_{0\leq\sigma\leq t_{k}}d_{\epsilon}(m,\sigma),

(4.49)

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{d}(m)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{1\leq m\leq K}\epsilon^{-1}+\mathbf{1}_{m\geq K+1}\epsilon^{-1-\zeta}.

(4.50)

We note that

a^{\star}_{\epsilon}(0,k)=1\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad d^{\star}_{\epsilon}(0,k)=0.

(4.51)

Finally, recalling (
4.42
), for
m\geq 1
we define

\beta^{\star}_{\epsilon}(m,k)=\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)\sup_{0\leq\sigma\leq t_{k}}\beta_{\epsilon}(m,\sigma),

(4.52)

where
\beta_{\epsilon}(m,\sigma)=\beta_{\epsilon}(m,\sigma;0)
as defined in (
4.22
) and
where

\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)=\epsilon^{-1-\zeta}.

(4.53)

We shall consider
m\leq N^{*}
where
N^{*}
is defined in (
2.33
).
By Corollary
2.17
, we have, for any positive integer
m\geq N^{*}+1
and
0<t\leq T
, that

a_{\epsilon}(m,t)\lesssim\epsilon^{1+\zeta}\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad d_{\epsilon}(m,t)\lesssim\epsilon^{1+\zeta}.

(4.54)

For
1\leq m\leq N^{*}
, we also define

\displaystyle\Gamma_{a}^{\epsilon}(m)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{m=1,2}\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}+\mathbf{1}_{3\leq m\leq K}\epsilon+\mathbf{1}_{m\geq K+1}\epsilon^{1+\zeta};

(4.55)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{d}(m)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{1\leq m\leq K}\epsilon+\mathbf{1}_{m\geq K+1}\epsilon^{1+\zeta}.

(4.56)

Finally, for any
m\geq 1

\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)=\epsilon^{1+\zeta}.

(4.57)

For
t^{*}=(k-1)\Delta
,
k=1,\dots,l+1
, and
1\leq m\leq N^{*}
, by Lemma
4.10
, the integral inequalities for
a^{*}_{\epsilon}(m,k)
take the form:

\displaystyle a^{*}_{\epsilon}(m,k)

\displaystyle\lesssim a^{*}_{\epsilon}(m,k-1)

(4.58)

\displaystyle+\Delta^{\zeta}\sum_{q=1}^{K}\;\;\;\sum_{p=2}^{\min\{m,K\}}\,\underbrace{\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\Gamma_{a}(m-p+q)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)}_{\leq\epsilon^{1-5\zeta}}a^{*}_{\epsilon}(m-p+q,k)\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}m-p+q\leq N^{*},\\
m\geq 2\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle+\Delta^{\zeta}\sum_{q=1}^{K}\;\;\;\sum_{p=2}^{\min\{m,K\}}\,\underbrace{\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)\epsilon^{1+\zeta}}_{\leq\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}}\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}m-p+q\geq N^{*}+1,\\
m\geq 2\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle+{\mathbf{1}_{m\geq 2}\Delta^{\zeta}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p=2,\\
p\neq m\end{subarray}}^{\min\{m,K\}}\underbrace{\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(m-p)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)}_{\leq\epsilon^{1-5\zeta}}\,a^{*}_{\epsilon}(m-p,k)}

\displaystyle+\Delta^{1/2}a^{*}_{\epsilon}(m,k)

\displaystyle+\Delta^{1/2}\sum_{q=2}^{K}\,\underbrace{\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(m-1+q)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)}_{\leq 1}a^{*}_{\epsilon}(m-1+q,k)\mathbf{1}_{m-1+q\leq N^{*}}

\displaystyle+\Delta^{1/2}\sum_{q=2}^{K}\,\underbrace{\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)\epsilon^{1+\zeta}}_{\leq 1}\mathbf{1}_{m-1+q\geq N^{*}+1}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{m=2}\underbrace{\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)}_{=1}\Delta^{\zeta}+\mathbf{1}_{3\leq m\leq K}\underbrace{\epsilon\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)}_{=1}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{m=3}\underbrace{\epsilon\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)}_{=1}\left(\Delta^{1/2}+\Delta^{1/2+\zeta}\right)+{\mathbf{1}_{m\geq 4}\underbrace{\epsilon^{2-4\zeta}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)}_{\epsilon^{1-5\zeta}}\Delta^{\zeta}}

\displaystyle+{\bf 1}_{m\geq 2}\left(\Delta^{1/2}+\Delta^{1/2-\zeta}\right)\underbrace{\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m-1)}_{\leq 1}\beta^{*}_{\epsilon}(m-1,k)

\displaystyle+{\bf 1}_{m=2}\left(\Delta^{1/2}+\Delta^{1/4}\right)\underbrace{\epsilon\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)}_{=\epsilon^{2\zeta}}

\displaystyle+{{\bf 1}_{m\geq 3}\left(\Delta^{1/2}+\Delta^{1/4}\right)\underbrace{\epsilon\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(m-2)}_{\leq\epsilon^{1-3\zeta}}a^{*}_{\epsilon}(m-2,k)},

where we have also included the estimates of the underbraced terms, whose derivation is explained later in the proof.
The above inequality is constructed as follows. We first change variables
t-\lambda\rightarrow\lambda
, take the supremum with respect to time of
a_{\epsilon}(m,\lambda)
and of
\beta_{\epsilon}(m-1,k\Delta-\lambda)
, allowing these terms to be pulled out of the time integrals. Next, we multiply and divide by the corresponding powers of
\epsilon
. The factors of
\epsilon
with negative exponents are absorbed into the terms
a^{*}_{\epsilon}
and
\beta^{*}_{\epsilon}
on the right-hand side, while the factors with positive exponents are collected into
\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}
and
\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\beta}
, respectively. Since
a^{*}_{\epsilon}
also appears on the left-hand side, all terms on the right-hand side are multiplied by
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)
. E.g. to obtain

{\bf 1}_{m\geq 2}\left(\Delta^{1/2}+\Delta^{1/2-\zeta}\right)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m-1)\beta^{*}_{\epsilon}(m-1,k),

we worked as follows:

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{m\geq 2}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)\int_{(k-1)\Delta}^{k\Delta}d\lambda\left(\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/2+\zeta}}\right)\underbrace{\epsilon^{1+\zeta}}_{=\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m-1)}\underbrace{\epsilon^{-1-\zeta}\sup_{0\leq\sigma\leq k\Delta}\beta_{\epsilon}(m-1,\sigma)}_{=\beta^{*}_{\epsilon}(m-1,k)}.

All underbraced terms in (
4.58
) that multiply
a^{*}_{\epsilon}
, are bounded uniformly in
\epsilon
as follows: we use the worst–case bounds, namely

\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(\cdot)\leq\epsilon^{\,1-2\zeta}
and

\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(\cdot)\leq\epsilon^{-1-\zeta}
, whenever the arguments of

\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}
permit such estimates, and by (
4.54
), for
m\geq 2

\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)\epsilon^{1+\zeta}\mathbf{1}_{m-p+q\geq N^{*}+1}\leq\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\;\;\textrm{ and }\;\;\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)\epsilon^{1+\zeta}\mathbf{1}_{m-1+q\geq N^{*}+1}\leq 1

(4.59)

and thus these terms become smaller than 1 since
\epsilon<1
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(m-p+q)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\epsilon^{-1-\zeta}=\epsilon^{1-5\zeta}.

Since
m-1+q\geq m
for
q\geq 2
,
\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(m-1+q)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)<1
when
\epsilon<1
. Finally,
\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m-1)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)\leq\epsilon^{1+\zeta}\epsilon^{-1-\zeta}=1
and
\epsilon\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(m-2)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)\leq\epsilon\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\epsilon^{-1-\zeta}=\epsilon^{1-3\zeta}
. We omit the estimation of the remaining terms, as it is straightforward to obtain them and is already indicated in the underbraced expressions. Also
\Delta
’s in each term of (
4.58
) have a positive exponent.
We also observe that the integral inequality for
d^{\star}_{\epsilon}(m,k)
, thanks to Lemma
4.12
, can be written as

\displaystyle d^{*}_{\epsilon}(m,k)\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{2\leq m\leq K}\epsilon\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{d}(m)+\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{d}(m)\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)\beta^{*}_{\epsilon}(m,k).

(4.60)

Finally, from (
4.46
), the integral inequality for
\beta_{\epsilon}(n,t)
can be estimated as

\displaystyle\beta^{*}_{\epsilon}(m,k)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\Delta^{-1/2}\underbrace{\epsilon\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)}_{\leq\epsilon^{1-3\zeta}}a_{\epsilon}^{*}(m,k-1)

(4.61)

\displaystyle+\Delta^{\zeta}\sum_{q=1}^{K}\;\;\;\sum_{p=2}^{\min\{m,K\}}\,\underbrace{\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(m-p+q)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)}_{\leq\epsilon^{1-5\zeta}}a^{*}_{\epsilon}(m-p+q,k)\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}m-p+q\leq N^{*},\\
m\geq 2\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle+\Delta^{\zeta}\sum_{q=1}^{K}\;\;\;\sum_{p=2}^{\min\{m,K\}}\,\underbrace{\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)\epsilon^{1+\zeta}}_{\leq\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}}\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}m-p+q\geq N^{*}+1,\\
m\geq 2\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{m\geq 2}\Delta^{\zeta}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p=2,\\
p\neq m\end{subarray}}^{\min\{m,K\}}\underbrace{\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(m-p)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)}_{\leq\epsilon^{1-5\zeta}}\,a^{{*}}_{\epsilon}(m-p,k)

\displaystyle+\Delta^{\zeta}\underbrace{\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(m)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)}_{\leq\epsilon^{1-5\zeta}}a^{*}_{\epsilon}(m,k)

\displaystyle+\Delta^{\zeta}\sum_{q=2}^{K}\underbrace{\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(m-1+q)\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)}_{\leq\epsilon^{1-5\zeta}}a^{*}_{\epsilon}(m-1+q,k)\mathbf{1}_{m-1+q\leq N^{*}}

\displaystyle+\Delta^{\zeta}\sum_{q=2}^{K}\,\underbrace{\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)\epsilon^{1+\zeta}}_{\leq\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}}\mathbf{1}_{m-1+q\geq N^{*}+1}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{m\geq 2}\Delta^{\zeta}\underbrace{\epsilon^{3/2-2\zeta}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(m-2)}_{\leq\epsilon^{3/2-5\zeta}}a^{*}_{\epsilon}(m-2,k)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{m\geq 2}\Delta^{\zeta}\underbrace{\epsilon^{1-3\zeta}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{d}(m-1)}_{\leq\epsilon^{1-4\zeta}}d^{*}_{\epsilon}(m-1,k).

Because the product of
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(m)
with any of
\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{a}(\cdot)
,
\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{\beta}(\cdot)
, or
\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{d}(\cdot)
may grow as fast as
\epsilon^{-3\zeta}
, while all terms are multiplied by
\epsilon^{1-x}
with
x\in\{0,-2\zeta,-\frac{1}{2}+2\zeta,-3\zeta\}
, it follows that each coefficient remains bounded by a positive power of
\epsilon
, and hence is uniformly bounded by 1 when
\epsilon<1
. By defining the vectors

\displaystyle\underline{x}(k)

\displaystyle=[a^{*}_{\epsilon}(1,k)\,\cdots a^{*}_{\epsilon}(N^{*},k)\,\,d^{*}_{\epsilon}(1,k)\,\cdots\,d^{*}_{\epsilon}(N^{*},k)\,\beta^{*}_{\epsilon}(1,k)\,\cdots\,\beta^{*}_{\epsilon}(N^{*},k)]^{T}

and

\displaystyle u_{\epsilon,\Delta}(k)=[u_{\epsilon,\Delta}(1,k)\,\cdots\,u_{\epsilon,\Delta}(3N^{*},k)]^{T}

for
k=1,\dots,l+1
, the vector version of (
4.58
),(
4.60
),(
4.61
) can be written as

\underline{x}(k)\leq A_{\epsilon,\Delta}\,\underline{x}(k-1)+\Lambda_{\epsilon,\Delta}(k)\underline{x}(k)+u_{\epsilon,\Delta}(k),\;\;\;\;k=1,\dots,l+1.

(4.62)

The elements of
A_{\epsilon,\Delta}
are given by

A_{\varepsilon,\Delta}(i,j)=\begin{cases}1,&1\leq i=j\leq N^{*},\\[5.69054pt]
\Delta^{-1/2}\,\varepsilon\,\Gamma_{a}^{\varepsilon}(m)\,\tilde{\Gamma}_{\beta}^{\varepsilon}(m),&i=2N^{*}+j,\;1\leq j\leq N^{*},\\[2.84526pt]
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

The elements of the matrix
\Lambda_{\epsilon,\Delta}(k)
, which are all bounded by a constant (because are bounded either by a positive power of
\Delta
, or by a positive power of
\epsilon
multiplied by a positive power of
\Delta
), can be figured out in a similar manner, and we leave those details to the reader. Finally, the matrix
u_{\epsilon,\Delta}(k)
consists of all terms in
(
4.58
), (
4.60
) and (
4.61
)
that do not involve
a^{*}_{\epsilon}
,
d^{*}_{\epsilon}
, or
\beta^{*}_{\epsilon}
,
and are uniformly bounded in
\epsilon
as well.
We choose
\Delta
such that

\|\Lambda_{\epsilon,\Delta}(k)\|_{\infty}<\frac{1}{2},

where
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
denotes the matrix norm defined as the maximum absolute row sum.
More generally, we may also choose
\Delta
such that

\|\Lambda_{\epsilon,\Delta}(k)\|_{\max}\leq C
for some constant
C<1
. For every
k=1,\dots,l+1
, the matrices
\Lambda_{\epsilon,\Delta}(k)
and
u_{\epsilon,\Delta}(k)

are uniformly bounded by
1/2
and some constant
c
, respectively, independently of
\epsilon
.
Consequently, all components of
\underline{x}(k)
are uniformly bounded in
\epsilon
for all
k=1,\dots,l+1
,
which proves the result.

Corollary 4.13
.

Let
K\geq 2
be a fixed integer number and
\zeta>0
. For any initial product measure
\mu^{\epsilon}
satisfying (
2.21
), and time
0<t\leq T

d_{\epsilon}(n,t)\lesssim\begin{cases}\epsilon,&\;1\leq n\leq K\\
\epsilon^{1+\zeta},&n\geq K+1,\end{cases}

(4.63)

and

\beta_{\epsilon}(n,t)\lesssim\epsilon^{1+\zeta}.

This corollary is the same as Corollary
2.22
expressed in terms of
d_{\epsilon}
including also the estimate for
\beta_{\epsilon}
. We do not present the proof as it follows easily from the proof of the previous result.

4.2
Further results on space
v
-functions

Before we move on to estimating the space-time
v
-function, we anticipate that the results we have previously obtained for the space
v
-function have to be refined in such a way that the bounds in
\epsilon
improve when estimating the space time function. Motivated by the integral inequality for the space-time
v
-function, given in Definition
2.23
, namely

\displaystyle v^{\epsilon,m,n}_{s,r}(\underline{y},\underline{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{w}}\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}(\underline{x}\overset{r-s}{\longrightarrow}\underline{w})v^{\epsilon,m,n}_{s,s}(\underline{w},\underline{y})+\int_{s}^{r}d\lambda\,\sum_{\underline{w}}\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}(\underline{x}\overset{r-\lambda}{\longrightarrow}\underline{w})(C_{\epsilon}v^{\epsilon,m,n}_{s,\lambda})(\underline{y},\underline{w}),

which is discussed in detail in Section
5
, we aim to improve the obtained estimates with respect to
\epsilon
for the space
v
-function. In particular, a better estimate of the initial condition, which depends on the space
v
-function but also on the transition probability of the stirring process,

\sum_{\underline{w}}\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}(\underline{x}\overset{r-s}{\longrightarrow}\underline{w})v^{\epsilon,m,n}_{s,s}(\underline{w},\underline{y})

is required. Our strategy is then to split the above sum into the cases
\underline{w}=\underline{y}
and
\underline{w}\neq\underline{y}
, the latter includes the case where
|\underline{w}\cap\underline{y}|=0
(i.e., none of the labeled particles in
\underline{w}
occupy the same site as any of the particles in
\underline{y}
) and, from Liggett’s inequality given in (
4.1
), it can be bounded from above by

\sum_{\pi\in S_{n}}\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{w}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{n}):\\
|\underline{w}\cap\underline{y}|=0\end{subarray}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(x_{\pi(i)},w_{i})\,|v_{n+m}^{\epsilon}(\underline{w}\cup\underline{y},s)|.

In Lemma
4.15
, we show that, for general times, the sum of products of transition probabilities multiplying the
v
’s, i.e., for any
\pi\in S_{n}
,

\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{w}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{n}):\\
|\underline{w}\cap\underline{y}|=0\end{subarray}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}P_{\tau}^{(\epsilon)}(x_{\pi(i)},w_{i})\,|v_{n+m}^{\epsilon}(\underline{w}\cup\underline{y},t)|,\quad\tau\neq t

improves the order in
\epsilon
by a factor of
\epsilon^{2\zeta}
for
n+m=2
and by
\epsilon^{\zeta}
for
4\leq n+m\leq K
. For the remaining cases of
n+m
, the order in
\epsilon
remains unchanged, meaning that we obtain a bound of order
\epsilon
when
n+m=2,3
, and of order
\epsilon^{1+\zeta}
when
n+m\geq K+1
.

We begin with Corollary
4.14
, in which we summarize the integral inequalities for
v_{n}^{\epsilon}
in a form that preserves those terms estimated by
\epsilon
to a positive power less than or equal to 1 in their original form, while by using Theorem
2.19
, the remaining terms are already bounded by
\epsilon^{1+\zeta}
and thus require no further treatment.

Corollary 4.14
.

Let
2\leq K\leq N
be a fixed integer number and
\zeta>0
. Let also

\mu^{\epsilon}
be any initial measure satisfying (
2.21
),
0<t\leq T
and
2\leq n\leq N^{*}-1
, where
N^{*}
is defined in (
2.33
). Then, the following bound holds:

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x},t)|\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{n=2,3}\epsilon T^{1/2}+\mathbf{1}_{4\leq n\leq N^{*}-1}\epsilon^{1+\zeta}T^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\mathbf{1}_{n\leq N^{*}-1}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j}(\lambda)\in I_{+}}|v_{n}^{\epsilon}\left(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)\cup z^{\prime},t-\lambda\right)|\Big]\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\underline{z}^{\prime}\subset I_{+},q\geq 2}\mathbf{1}_{n+q\leq N^{*}-1}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[|v_{n-1+q}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\Big]\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda)=I^{n}_{+}}\right].

(4.64)

Proof.

The result follows from (
4.44
) with
t^{*}=0
, Corollary
4.13
and Theorem
2.19
. More precisely, taking (
4.44
) with
t^{*}=0
, and noting that
a_{\epsilon}(n,0)=0
, we get the bound:

\displaystyle|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x},t)|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1-\zeta}}\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}

\displaystyle+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j}(\lambda)\in I_{+}}|v_{n}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j)}{(\lambda)}\cup z^{\prime},t-\lambda)|\Big]\right]

\displaystyle+\int_{t^{*}}^{t}d\lambda\;\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{z}^{\prime}\subset I_{+},\\
q\geq 2\end{subarray}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\left[|v_{n-1+q}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\Big]\right]

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda\;\phi_{0}(\lambda)\epsilon^{1+\zeta}+\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda\;\phi_{2}^{\epsilon}(\lambda)\epsilon^{1-2\zeta}\,.

And now a simple computation allows to conclude the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.15
.

We fix an integer number
K
such that
2\leq K\leq N
. Let
\underline{x}\in\Lambda_{N}^{\neq,n}
and
\underline{y}\in\Lambda_{N}^{\neq,m}
such that
2\leq n+m\leq N^{*}-1
. For any
\,0<\tau,t\leq T
, with
t\neq\tau
, and for any
\pi\in S_{n}
, the following bound holds:

\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{w}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{n}):\\
|\underline{w}\,\cap\ \underline{y}|=0\end{subarray}}}\;\;\prod_{i=1}^{n}P_{\tau}^{(\epsilon)}(x_{\pi(i)},w_{i})|v_{n+m}^{\epsilon}(\underline{w},\underline{y},t)|\lesssim\begin{cases}\epsilon+\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}},&\;\;m=1\textrm{ and }n=1,2\\
\epsilon^{1+\zeta}+\frac{\epsilon}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}},&\;\;4\leq n+m\leq N^{*}-1\end{cases}

(4.65)

where the constant in
\lesssim
depends on
N^{*},K,T
and it is given by
N^{*}e^{(N^{*})^{3}K^{4}\pi T}N^{*}K^{N^{*}}T^{N^{*}/2}
.

Remark 4.16
.

Note that the bound in (
4.65
) is independent of
t
. This is because
t
appears only through a positive exponent, which we may uniformly control by replacing
t
with its maximal value
T
. Consequently, the resulting estimate depends on
T
but not on
t\in[0,T]
. For our convenience in the calculations later, we call

D^{\epsilon}_{n+m}(\tau):=\begin{cases}\epsilon+\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}},&\;\;m=1\textrm{ and }n=1,2\\
\epsilon^{1+\zeta}+\frac{\epsilon}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}},&\;\;4\leq n+m\leq N^{*}-1.\end{cases}

(4.66)

Proof.

We note that we first prove the lemma for the identity permutation
\pi(i)=i
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
in (
4.65
).
The proof then extends directly to any
\pi\in S_{n}
, as the same arguments apply. Let
(n,m)
such that
n,m\geq 1
and
n+m=N^{*}-1
. We start by working only with
v^{\epsilon}_{N^{*}-1}
, without considering the sum of product of probabilities given in (
4.65
). To simplify the next formulas we denote
\underline{u}=\underline{w}\cup\underline{y}
. It is easy to check that
N^{*}-1>4
(since
N^{*}
should be large to satisfy (
2.33
)). By Corollary
4.14

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt|v_{N^{*}-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{u},t)|\lesssim\epsilon^{1+\zeta}T^{1/2}+\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda_{1}\;\epsilon^{-1}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\left[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda_{1})=I^{N^{*}-1}_{+}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt+\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{N^{*}-1}\sum_{z_{1}^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda_{1}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{1}}(\lambda_{1})\in I_{+}}\underbrace{|v_{N^{*}-1}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j_{1})}(\lambda_{1})\cup z_{1}^{\prime},t-\lambda_{1}\big)|}_{\textrm{we bound it using \eqref{13.1.111 with gammas 2_2} with $t-\lambda_{1}$}}\Big],

(4.67)

where the third line in (
4.14
) is estimated as
\epsilon^{1+\zeta}T^{\zeta}
because the degree of all
v
’s included in this term satisfy
n-1+q\geq N^{*}
for
q\geq 2
, and thus (
2.34
) applies. This argument cannot be applied to
|v_{N^{*}-1}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda_{1})\cup z^{\prime},t-\lambda_{1}\big)|
appearing in the second line of (
4.14
) since its degree satisfies
N^{*}-1<N^{*}
. We handle this term as follows: we eliminate
|v_{N^{*}-1}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda_{1})\cup z^{\prime},t-\lambda_{1}\big)|
from (
4.2
) by bounding it using the bound (
4.2
). This introduces the sum of the following additional terms to (
4.2
):

\bullet
Additional term 1:

\displaystyle K\epsilon^{1+\zeta}T^{1/2}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{N^{*}-1}\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda_{1}\epsilon^{-1}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{1}}(\lambda_{1})\in I_{+}}\right].

Note that the factor
K
is coming from the sum
\sum_{z_{1}^{\prime}\in I_{+}}
appearing in (
4.2
).

\bullet
Additional term 2:

\displaystyle\epsilon^{-2}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{N^{*}-1}\sum_{z_{1}^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda_{1}\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{t}d\lambda_{2}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{1}}(\lambda_{1})\in I_{+}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{1})}(\lambda_{1})}\left[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda_{2})=I^{N^{*}-1}_{+}}\right]\right].

\bullet
Additional term 3:

\displaystyle\epsilon^{-2}\!\!\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{N^{*}-1}\sum_{z_{1}^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\sum_{j_{2}=1}^{N^{*}-1}\sum_{z_{2}^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\int_{0}^{t}\!\!\!\!d\lambda_{1}\!\!\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{t}\!d\lambda_{2}\,\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{1}}(\lambda_{1})\in I_{+}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{1})}(\lambda_{1}),\,z_{1}^{\prime}}[\underbrace{|v_{N^{*}-1}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j_{2})}(\lambda_{2})\cup z^{\prime}_{2},t-\lambda_{2}\big)|}_{\textrm{we bound it using \eqref{13.1.111 with gammas 2_2} for $t-\lambda_{2}$}}]].

By iterating this procedure (i.e., every time bounding
|v_{N^{*}-1}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j_{k})}(\lambda_{k})\cup z^{\prime}_{k},t-\lambda_{k}\big)|
by itself in the additional term 3), we obtain an infinite series whose
k
-th term is the sum of the following terms:

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\epsilon^{1+\zeta}T^{1/2}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{N^{*}-1}\sum_{z_{1}^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\cdots\sum_{j_{k}=1}^{N^{*}-1}\sum_{z_{k}^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda_{1}\cdots\int_{\lambda_{k-1}}^{t}d\lambda_{k}

\displaystyle\times\epsilon^{-k}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{1}}(\lambda_{1})\in I_{+}}\left[\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{1})}(\lambda_{1}),\,z_{1}^{\prime}}\left[\cdots\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{k-1})}(\lambda_{k-1}),\,z_{k-1}^{\prime}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{k}}(\lambda_{k})\in I_{+}}\right]\cdots\right]\right]\right]

(4.68)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\epsilon^{-k}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{N^{*}-1}\sum_{z_{1}^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\cdots\sum_{j_{k}=1}^{N^{*}-1}\sum_{z_{k}^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda_{1}\cdots\int_{\lambda_{k}}^{t}d\lambda_{k+1}\epsilon^{-1}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\times\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{1}}(\lambda_{1})\in I_{+}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{1})}(\lambda_{1}),\,z_{1}^{\prime}}\left[\cdots\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{k}}(\lambda_{k})\in I_{+}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{k})}(\lambda_{k}),\,z_{k}^{\prime}}\left[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda_{k+1})=I^{N^{*}-1}_{+}}\right]\right]\cdots\right]\right].

(4.69)

Note that in the configurations appearing in the index in the expectations above, and in those used later in the proof, we omit the symbol “
\cup
” and replace by comma, e.g.
\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{k})}(\lambda_{k}),\,z_{k}^{\prime}}[\cdot]=\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{k})}(\lambda_{k})\,\cup\,z_{k}^{\prime}}[\cdot]
. The first term (
4.81
) is estimated as follows: we start with estimating the last expectation

\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{k-1})}(\lambda_{k-1}),\,z_{k-1}^{\prime}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{k}}(\lambda_{k})\in I_{+}}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k-1})]^{1/2}+1}.

Similarly,

\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{k-2})}(\lambda_{k-2}),\,z_{k-2}^{\prime}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{k-1}}(\lambda_{k-1})\in I_{+}}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda_{k-1}-\lambda_{k-2})]^{1/2}+1}.

By keep estimating in the same manner, the first term is eventually bounded by

[K(N^{*}-1)]^{k}\epsilon^{1+\zeta}T^{1/2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{1}}}\;d\lambda_{1}\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}}}d\lambda_{2}\;\dots\int_{\lambda_{k-1}}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k-1}}}d\lambda_{k}.

(4.70)

The remaining part is devoted to estimating the second term (
4.2
), making use of

\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{w}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{n}):\\
|\underline{w}\,\cap\ \underline{y}|=0\end{subarray}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\tau}(x_{i},w_{i}),

(4.71)

(which was not used for the estimation of the first term (
4.81
), as we simply bounded (
4.71
) by 1) and showing that it is bounded by

\left((N^{*}-1)K^{2}\right)^{k}\frac{\epsilon T}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{1}}}\;d\lambda_{1}\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}}}d\lambda_{2}\;\dots\int_{\lambda_{k-1}}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k-1}}}d\lambda_{k}.

(4.72)

We call
a_{0}(t):=1
and for
k\geq 1
with
0=\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}<\dots<\lambda_{k}<t

a_{k}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{1}}}\;d\lambda_{1}\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}}}d\lambda_{2}\;\dots\int_{\lambda_{k-1}}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k-1}}}d\lambda_{k}.

(4.73)

Then, by (
4.70
) and (
4.72
), it is immediate that when
n+m=N^{*}-1
and
\tau\neq t

\displaystyle\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{w}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{n}):\\
|\underline{w}\,\cap\ \underline{y}|=0\end{subarray}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\tau}(x_{i},w_{i})|v_{N^{*}-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{u},t)|

(4.74)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\left(\epsilon^{1+\zeta}T^{1/2}+\frac{\epsilon}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}}\right)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left((N^{*}-1)K^{2}\right)^{k}a_{k}(t).

(4.75)

By Lemma 10.4 in
[
6
]
, the series in last display is bounded by
e^{(N^{*}-1)^{2}K^{4}\pi t}
, and thus the right-hand side of (
4.74
) is bounded by

\underbrace{e^{(N^{*})^{2}K^{4}\pi T}T^{1/2}}_{\textrm{constant for $n+m=N^{*}-1$}}\left(\epsilon^{1+\zeta}+\frac{\epsilon}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\right),

(4.76)

where we used that
e^{(N^{*}-1)^{2}K^{4}\pi T}\leq e^{(N^{*})^{2}K^{4}\pi T}
.

Now we prove (
4.2
). All calculations are performed by keeping track of the history of each particle and the events happened at
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{k+1}
, i.e.

\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{1}}(\lambda_{1})\in I_{+}}\left[\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{1})}(\lambda_{1}),\,z_{1}^{\prime}}\left[\cdots\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{k-1})}(\lambda_{k-1}),\,z_{k-1}^{\prime}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{k}}(\lambda_{k})\in I_{+}}\right]\cdots\right]\right]\right],

where starting from the configuration
\underline{u}
, at time
\lambda_{1}
, the particle labeled by
j_{1}
reaches the boundary, dies, and a new particle is born at the site
z_{1}^{\prime}\in I_{+}
. The particles in the resulting configuration
(\underline{x}^{(j_{1})}(\lambda_{1}),z_{1}^{\prime})
then move within the system until, at time
\lambda_{2}
, the particle labeled by
j_{2}
reaches the boundary, dies, and a new particle is born at the site
z_{2}^{\prime}\in I_{+}
. The particles continue evolving in this manner until, at time
\lambda_{k+1}
, all particles in the configuration
(\underline{x}^{(j_{k})}(\lambda_{k}),z_{k}^{\prime})
occupy the last
N^{*}-1
sites at the boundary. All said events are then estimated using Gaussian kernels, meaning (
B.5
) and (
B.11
).

We present the calculations for
k=2
(i.e., 2-iterations) and easily can be adopted for general
k
. By developing the expectation, using Liggett’s inequality given in (
C.7
) and Kolmogorov‘s identity, we obtain: for simplicity, let us call the initial configuration
\underline{u}=(\underline{w},\underline{y})
, then we first compute

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07182pt\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{1}}(\lambda_{1})\in I_{+}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{1})}(\lambda_{1}),\,z_{1}^{\prime}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{2}}(\lambda_{2})\in I_{+}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{2})}(\lambda_{2}),\,z_{2}^{\prime}}\left[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda_{3})=I^{N^{*}-1}_{+}}\right]\right]\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{z_{1}\in I_{+}}\sum_{z_{2}\in I_{+}}\sum_{\pi\in S_{n+m}}\prod_{i\neq j_{1},j_{2}}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda_{3}}(u_{\pi(i)},N-N^{*}+i+1)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda_{3}-\lambda_{1}}(z_{1}^{\prime},N-N^{*}+j_{1}+1)P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda_{1}}(u_{\pi(j_{1})},z_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda_{3}-\lambda_{2}}(z_{2}^{\prime},N-N^{*}+j_{2}+1)P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda_{2}}(u_{\pi(j_{2})},z_{2}),

(4.77)

where
S_{n+m}
is the set of permutations of
\{1,\dots,n+m\}
, and
z_{1},z_{2}
come from the fact that we write

\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{1}}(\lambda_{1})\in I_{+}}=\sum_{z_{1}\in I_{+}}\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{1}}(\lambda_{1})=z_{1}}\quad\textrm{ and }\quad\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{2}}(\lambda_{2})\in I_{+}}=\sum_{z_{2}\in I_{+}}\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{2}}(\lambda_{2})=z_{2}}.

Recalling that
\underline{u}=\underline{w}\,\cup\,\underline{y}
, the
n
first particles are
\underline{w}
. For any
\pi\in S_{n+m}
, we focus on
\{u_{\pi(i)}:i\}=\{w_{1},\dots,w_{n}\}
. For all these
u_{\pi(i)}
, we use that

\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{w}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{n}):\\
|\underline{w}\,\cap\ \underline{y}|=0\end{subarray}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(P_{\tau}^{(\epsilon)}(x_{i},w_{i})P^{(\epsilon)}_{s}(w_{i},\cdot)\right)\leq\prod_{i=1}^{n}P^{(\epsilon)}_{s+\tau}(x_{i},\cdot),

where
s
can be any of
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
and
\lambda_{3}
and
"\cdot"=N-N^{*}+i-1,z_{1},z_{2}
.
Overall, e.g.,
u_{\pi(j_{1})}\in\underline{x}
and
u_{\pi(j_{2})}\notin\underline{x}

\displaystyle\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{w}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{n}):\\
|\underline{w}\,\cap\ \underline{y}|=0\end{subarray}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}P_{\tau}^{(\epsilon)}(x_{i},w_{i})\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{1}}(\lambda_{1})\in I_{+}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{1})}(\lambda_{1}),\,z_{1}^{\prime}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j_{2}}(\lambda_{2})\in I_{+}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{x}^{(j_{2})}(\lambda_{2}),\,z_{2}^{\prime}}\left[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda_{3})=I^{N^{*}-1}_{+}}\right]\right]\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\tau+\lambda_{3})]^{(n-1)/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda_{3}]^{(m-1)/2}+1}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda_{3}-\lambda_{1})]^{1/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda_{3}-\lambda_{2})]^{1/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\tau+\lambda_{1})]^{1/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda_{2}]^{1/2}+1}.

(4.78)

Since
0<\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}<t
, we use the following inequalities:

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}>\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1};\quad\tau+\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{1};\quad\lambda_{3}-\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{3}-\lambda_{2};\quad\tau+\lambda_{3}>\tau;\quad\tau+\lambda_{3}>\lambda_{3}>\lambda_{3}-\lambda_{2}.

(4.79)

By using (
4.79
), we bound the first line of the right-hand side in (
4.2
) by

\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\tau+\lambda_{3})]^{(n-1)/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda_{3}]^{(m-1)/2}+1}\leq\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda_{3}-\lambda_{2})]^{(n+m-3)/2}+1},

while the second line of (
4.2
) is bounded by

\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda_{3}-\lambda_{2})]+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda_{1})]^{1/2}}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})]^{1/2}}.

Putting them together, we obtain
the next bound for the right-hand side of (
4.2
):

\left(\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})]^{1/2}}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda_{1}]^{1/2}}\right)\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda_{3}-\lambda_{2})]^{(n+m-1)/2}+1}.

For any
j_{1},j_{2}\in\{1,\dots,N^{*}-1\}
, we can always obtain a similar bound with products of transition probabilities as in (
4.2
) and then adjust
\lambda
’s similar to (
4.79
), leading to the following bound

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{N^{*}-1}\sum_{z_{1},z_{2}\in I_{+}}\sum_{z_{1}^{\prime},z_{2}^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda_{1}\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{t}d\lambda_{2}\prod_{l=1}^{2}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{l-1})^{1/2}}}_{=a_{2}(t)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\int_{\lambda_{2}}^{t}d\lambda_{3}\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda_{3}-\lambda_{2})]^{(N^{*}-2)/2}+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left((N^{*}-1)K^{2}\right)^{2}a_{2}(t)\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\epsilon^{2},

(4.80)

where we use Remark
4.5
to estimate

\int_{\lambda_{2}}^{t}d\lambda_{3}\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}(\lambda_{3}-\lambda_{2})]^{(N^{*}-2)/2}+1}\lesssim\epsilon^{2},

because
N^{*}-2\geq 3
. Also, the constant
\left((N^{*}-1)K^{2}\right)^{2}
appearing in (
4.2
), comes from the double sum with respect to
j_{1},j_{2}
and the two double sums with respect
z_{1},z_{2}
and
z_{1}^{\prime},z_{2}^{\prime}
which gives us
(N^{*}-1)^{2}
an
K^{4}
in total, respectively.
For any
j_{1},\dots,j_{k}
and
k\in\mdmathbb{N}
, we can always obtain a similar bound with products of transition probabilities as in (
4.2
) and then adjust
\lambda
’s similar to (
4.79
), leading to the following bound

\displaystyle\left((N^{*}-1)K^{2}\right)^{k}a_{k}(t)\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\epsilon,

(4.81)

for the second term (
4.2
).

Let
(n,m)
such that
n,m\geq 1
and
n+m=N^{*}-2
. The integral inequality for
v_{N^{*}-2}^{\epsilon}
is given by Corollary
4.14
. The term in the third line of (
4.58
) includes

\epsilon^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{j=1}^{N^{*}-2}\;\;\sum_{\underline{z}^{\prime}\subset I_{+}:|\underline{z}^{\prime}|=2}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\Big[|v_{N^{*}-1}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\Big]

(4.82)

i.e., the terms with
|J|=1
and
|\underline{z}^{\prime}|=2
. We obtain a bound proportional to
\epsilon^{1+\zeta}
as follows: We observe that
for
j=1,\dots N^{*}-2
and by (
C.7
),
\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\Big[|v_{N^{*}-1}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\Big]
is bounded by

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{v}=(v_{1},\dots,v_{N^{*}-2}),\\
|\underline{v}\,\cap\underline{z}^{\prime}|=0\end{subarray}}\mathbf{P}_{\epsilon}(\underline{u}\overset{\lambda}{\rightarrow}z)v_{N^{*}-1}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{v}^{(j)}\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\lesssim\sum_{\pi\in S_{N^{*}-2}}\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{v}^{(j)},\\
|\underline{v}^{(j)}\,\cap\underline{z}^{\prime}|=0\end{subarray}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{N^{*}-3}P^{(\epsilon)}_{\lambda}(u_{\pi(i)},v_{i})|v_{N^{*}-1}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{v}^{(j)}\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|}_{\textrm{estimated by \eqref{a10.8**}}}\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\lesssim\underbrace{e^{(N^{*})^{2}K^{4}\pi T}T^{1/2}}_{\textrm{constant denoted by $C^{(N^{*}-1)}_{K,T,N^{*}}$}}\left(\epsilon^{1+\zeta}+\frac{\epsilon}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\right).\end{split}

The last inequality follows from (
4.76
) with
n=N^{*}-3
,
m=|\underline{z}^{\prime}|=2
,
\tau=\lambda
and
t-\lambda
in place of
t
. Thus (
4.82
) is bounded by

\epsilon^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\sum_{j=1}^{N^{*}-2}\;\;\sum_{\underline{z}^{\prime}\subset I_{+}:|\underline{z}^{\prime}|=2}e^{(N^{*})^{2}K^{4}\pi T}T^{1/2}\left(\epsilon^{1+\zeta}+\frac{\epsilon}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\right).

After integrating, we obtain the bound for the last display as

e^{(N^{*})^{2}K^{4}\pi T}(N^{*}-2){{K}\choose{2}}\left(\epsilon^{1+\zeta}T+\epsilon^{2-2\zeta}T^{1/2+\zeta}\right)\leq e^{(N^{*})^{2}K^{4}\pi T}N^{*}K^{2}T\,\epsilon^{1+\zeta}.

(4.83)

For the terms with
|J|=1
and
|\underline{z}^{\prime}|=1
, i.e.,

\epsilon^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda\sum_{j=1}^{N^{*}-2}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{x_{j}(\lambda)\in I_{+}}|v_{N^{*}-2}^{\epsilon}\big(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)\cup z^{\prime},t-\lambda\big)|\Big],

we build the infinite series described earlier in the proof. Each
k
-th term of

\int_{0}^{t}d\lambda\;\epsilon^{-1}\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\left[{\bf 1}_{\underline{x}(\lambda)=I^{N^{*}-1}_{+}}\right]

and the remaining terms, including (
4.82
) (all estimated as
\epsilon^{1+\zeta}
), are treated exactly as in the case
N^{*}-1
. Reminding that
\underline{u}=\underline{w}\,\cup\,\underline{y}
, overall, the left-hand side of (
4.65
) is bounded by

e^{(N^{*}-2)^{2}K^{4}\pi T}\left(2e^{(N^{*})^{2}K^{4}\pi T}N^{*}K^{2}T\,\epsilon^{1+\zeta}+\frac{\epsilon}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\right)

and since the above is bounded by
2e^{2(N^{*})^{2}K^{4}\pi T}N^{*}K^{2}T\left(\epsilon^{1+\zeta}+\frac{\epsilon}{[\epsilon^{-2}\lambda]^{1/2}+1}\right)
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{w}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{n}):\\
|\underline{w}\,\cap\ \underline{y}|=0\end{subarray}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}P_{\tau}^{(\epsilon)}(x_{i},w_{i})|v_{N^{*}-2}^{\epsilon}(\underline{u},t)|\leq\underbrace{2e^{(N^{*})^{2}K^{4}\pi T}N^{*}K^{2}T}_{\textrm{constant denoted by $C^{(N^{*}-2)}_{K,T,N^{*}}$ }}\left(\epsilon^{1+\zeta}+\frac{\epsilon}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}+1}\right).

We proceed in this manner for all pairs
(n,m)
with
n,m\geq 1
and

n+m=N^{*}-3,\,N^{*}-4,\,\dots\,,2

with the corresponding constant being equal to

C^{(n+m)}_{K,T,N^{*}}=(N^{*}-(n+m))e^{(N^{*}-(n+m))(N^{*})^{2}K^{4}\pi T}N^{*}K^{(N^{*}-(n+m))}T^{(N^{*}-(n+m))/2},

for
n+m=2,3,\cdots,N^{*}-2
. For example, when
n+m=N^{*}-3
, the method is the same as described above, but we must additionally estimate

\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\Big[\,|v_{N^{*}-1}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},\,t-\lambda)|\,\Big],\quad\mathbf{E}_{\epsilon,\underline{u}}\Big[\,|v_{N^{*}-2}^{\epsilon}(\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)\cup\underline{z}^{\prime},\,t-\lambda)|\,\Big],

where
|\underline{x}^{(j)}(\lambda)|=n+m-1=N^{*}-4
,
|\underline{z}^{\prime}|=3
and
|\underline{z}^{\prime}|=2
, respectively. Both are estimated by using
(
4.74
) and (
4.2
).

The proof for
n,m
with
n+m=3
follows the same steps described above. Due to
\epsilon T^{1/2}
in (
4.58
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{w}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{n}):\\
|\underline{w}\,\cap\ \underline{y}|=0\end{subarray}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}P_{\tau}^{(\epsilon)}(x_{i},w_{i})|v_{n+m}^{\epsilon}(\underline{u},t)|\lesssim C^{(n+m)}_{K,T,N^{*}}\left(\epsilon+\frac{1}{[\epsilon^{-2}\tau]^{1/2}}\epsilon\right).

The last case for
n=m=1
is handled similarly. We finish the proof by observing that

C^{(n+m)}_{K,T,N^{*}}\leq N^{*}e^{(N^{*})^{3}K^{4}\pi T}N^{*}K^{N^{*}}T^{N^{*}/2}.

∎

5
Estimates on space-time
v
-functions

Let
\underline{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\in\Lambda_{N}^{\neq,n}
and
\underline{y}=(y_{1},\dots,y_{m})\in\Lambda_{N}^{\neq,m}
, with
n,m\geq 1
. For any initial product measure
\mu^{\epsilon}
and times
0<s<r
, the space-time
v
-correlation function is given by (
2.37
).
At the earliest time
s
, the positions of the
m
particles are
\underline{y}=(y_{1},\dots,y_{m})
while at time
r
, the positions of the
n
particles are
\underline{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
. The subscript
s,r
indicates the times involved, ordered from smallest to largest. The superscript includes the parameter
\epsilon
, as well as the number of particles (i.e., the cardinality of the configurations
\underline{y}
and
\underline{x}
) at times
s
and
r
. The input of the space-time
v
-correlation function consists of the actual labeled configurations, where
\underline{y}
